<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the finite case of the Benson–Etingof–Ostrik conjecture: every finite symmetric tensor category over an algebraically closed field <i>k</i> of characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; 0 admits a <i>k</i>-linear exact faithful strong symmetric monoidal functor to a finite higher Verlinde category $`\mathop{\mathrm{Ver}}\nolimits _{p^n}(k)`$. The result includes characteristic two, and the level <i>n</i> may depend on the category.

- **claim-main-fiber-functor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every prime $p$, every algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p$, and every finite symmetric tensor category $\mathcal C$ over $k$, there are an integer $n\geq1$ and a fiber functor $$\mathcal C\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ver}_{p^n}(k).$$
  - Explanation: Every finite symmetric tensor category over an algebraically closed field of positive characteristic maps by an exact faithful strong symmetric monoidal functor into some finite higher Verlinde category. The level depends on the category.
- **lem-finite-conventions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-conventions)): Under the conventions of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the unit of $\mathcal C$ is simple and $\mathcal C$ has a projective generator $P$. The functor $\omega=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal C}(P,-)$ is exact and faithful, and $\mathcal C$ is equivalent as a $k$-linear abelian category to the category of finite-dimensional modules over a finite-dimensional $k$-algebra.
  - Explanation: Under the finite-category conventions the unit is simple, a projective generator exists, evaluation at it is exact and faithful, and the category is a module category of a finite-dimensional algebra.
- **lem-combine-functors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-combine-functors)): Let $\mathcal A_1,\ldots,\mathcal A_s,\mathcal Z$ be finite symmetric tensor categories. Exact $k$-linear symmetric tensor functors $F_i:\mathcal A_i\to\mathcal Z$ induce an exact $k$-linear symmetric tensor functor $$H:\mathcal A_1\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal A_s\to\mathcal Z, \qquad H(X_1\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes X_s)=\bigotimes_i F_i(X_i).$$ Deligne products of exact tensor functors are exact as well.
  - Explanation: Exact symmetric tensor functors out of several finite symmetric tensor categories into a common target combine into one exact symmetric tensor functor on their Deligne product.
- **thm-restricted-tilting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-restricted-tilting)): Let $r\geq1$, let $E_r=(\mathbb Z/p)^r$, and let $\iota:E_r\hookrightarrow U(k)$ be any embedding. Write $R_\iota$ for restriction along $\iota$. For every finite-dimensional $kE_r$-module $D$, there is a tilting $G$-module $T$ such that $$R_\iota(\operatorname{St}_{r-1})\otimes D\simeq R_\iota(T).$$
  - Explanation: For every embedding of the elementary abelian group into the upper unipotent subgroup, tensoring the restricted Steinberg module with any module gives the restriction of a tilting module.
- **prop-bounded-origin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-bounded-origin)): For every finite-dimensional $kE_r$-module $D$, there is a $G$-equivariant vector bundle $\mathcal M$ on $\mathbb A^2_k$ such that the stabilizer action on the fibre at $e_1=(1,0)$ restricts to $D$ along $\iota$. Every simple composition factor of the rational $G$-module $M_0=\mathcal M\|_0$ has highest weight less than $q$.
  - Explanation: Any module of the elementary abelian group is realised as a fibre of an equivariant vector bundle on the plane whose origin fibre has only small highest weights.
- **lem-bounded-tilting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-tilting)): Let $k$ be algebraically closed of characteristic $p>0$, let $m\geq0$, and let $M$ be a finite-dimensional rational $SL_2(k)$-module. If every simple composition factor of $M$ is $L(h)$ with $0\leq h<p^{m+1}$, then $\operatorname{St}_m\otimes M$ is tilting. Here $\operatorname{St}_0=k$.
  - Explanation: A module whose composition factors have highest weight below the stated bound becomes tilting after tensoring with the corresponding Steinberg module.
- **lem-formal-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-formal-comparison)): Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of arbitrary positive characteristic. Let $\mathcal N$ be a $G$-equivariant vector bundle on $V$, and suppose that its origin fibre $T=\mathcal N_0$ is a tilting $G$-module. Then $$\mathcal N_{e_1}\cong T\|_U$$ as rational $U$-modules over $k$.
  - Explanation: For an equivariant bundle with tilting origin fibre, the fibre at the first basis point is isomorphic to the restriction of the origin fibre to the unipotent subgroup.
- **lem-theta-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-theta-detection)): If $T$ is a tilting module and $R(T)$ is not projective, then $\Theta(R(T))\ne0$. Moreover, $\Theta$ annihilates every $E$-module induced from a proper subgroup of $E$.
  - Explanation: The functor Theta is nonzero on nonprojective restrictions of tilting modules and kills modules induced from proper subgroups.
- **lem-projective-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projective-evaluation)): For every projective $Q\in\mathcal C$ and every $E$-object $Z$ of $\mathcal C$, there is a natural isomorphism $$\label{eq:projective-evaluation} (\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal C}(Q,-)\boxtimes \operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal W})(\Theta_{\mathcal C}(Z)) \simeq\Theta(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal C}(Q,Z)).$$
  - Explanation: Evaluating the internal extension of Theta at a projective object agrees naturally with applying Theta to the Hom space.
- **prop-higher-exactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-higher-exactness)): If there are projective objects $P,Q\in\mathcal C$ for which $$\Theta(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal C}(Q,P^{\otimes q}))\ne0,$$ then $\Phi$ is exact.
  - Explanation: If Theta is nonzero on one such Hom space between projectives, the higher Frobenius functor is exact.
- **lem-norm-projectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-norm-projectivity)): Let $H$ be a finite $p$-group and $M$ a finite-dimensional $kH$-module. Then $M$ is projective if and only if $\nu_H(M)$ is an isomorphism. For an arbitrary finite group $G$, the norm map of every projective $kG$-module is an isomorphism.
  - Explanation: Over a finite p-group a module is projective exactly when its norm map is an isomorphism; projective modules over any finite group have isomorphic norm maps.
- **lem-internal-norm-products** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-internal-norm-products)): Let finite $p$-groups $H_1,\dots,H_s$ act on objects $X_1,\dots,X_s$ of $\mathcal C$, respectively. If each $\omega(X_i)$ is projective over $kH_i$, then $\omega(X_1\otimes\cdots\otimes X_s)$ is projective over $k[H_1\times\cdots\times H_s]$, for the independent actions on the tensor factors. Its restriction to every subgroup of that product is projective as well.
  - Explanation: Projectivity of the evaluated factors under independent p-group actions gives projectivity of the evaluated tensor product over the product group and all its subgroups.
- **prop-first-nonprojective** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-first-nonprojective)): There is a least integer $r\ge1$ for which $M(r)$ is not projective over $kE_r$. For this $r$, the restriction of $M(r)$ to every proper subgroup of $E_r$ is projective.
  - Explanation: A least rank exists at which the evaluated tensor-power module stops being projective, and it is projective on all proper subgroups.
- **lem-support-independent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-support-independent)): Suppose that $M$ is a nonprojective finite-dimensional $kE_r$-module and that $M\|_H$ is projective for every proper subgroup $H<E_r$. Every point $[\alpha_1:\cdots:\alpha_r]$ of $\operatorname{Supp}(M)$ has $\mathbb F_p$-linearly independent coordinates.
  - Explanation: Support points of such a minimal nonprojective module have coordinates that are linearly independent over the prime field.
- **lem-steinberg-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-steinberg-support)): Let $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r\in k$ be linearly independent over $\mathbb F_p$, and restrict $\mathrm{SL}_2$-representations along $$\iota_\lambda:E_r\longrightarrow\mathrm{SL}_2(k), \qquad g_i\longmapsto \begin{pmatrix}1&\lambda_i\\0&1\end{pmatrix}.$$ Write $R_\lambda$ for this restriction. Then $$\label{eq:steinberg-support} \operatorname{Supp}\big(R_\lambda(\operatorname{St}_{r-1})\big) =\left\{[\alpha]: \sum_i\alpha_i\lambda_i^{p^j}=0\quad(0\le j\le r-2)\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: The rank support of the restricted Steinberg module is cut out by Frobenius-twisted linear equations in the embedding parameters.
- **lem-moore-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-moore-selection)): Let $\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\in k$ be linearly independent over $\mathbb F_p$. There are $\mathbb F_p$-linearly independent $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r\in k$ such that $$\label{eq:selected-embedding} \sum_{i=1}^r\alpha_i\lambda_i^{p^j}=0 \qquad(0\le j\le r-2).$$ For $r=1$ the list of equations is empty.
  - Explanation: Given independent parameters, one can solve the twisted equations by independent parameters, using a kernel of a Moore-type matrix.
- **prop-support-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-support-selection)): Let $M$ be a nonprojective finite-dimensional $kE_r$-module whose restriction to every proper subgroup is projective. There is an embedding $\iota_\lambda:E_r\hookrightarrow\mathrm{SL}_2(k)$ into the upper unipotent subgroup such that $$R_\lambda(\operatorname{St}_{r-1})\otimes M \quad\text{is nonprojective}.$$
  - Explanation: One can choose the unipotent embedding so that the tensor product with the restricted Steinberg module stays nonprojective.
- **thm-exact-higher-frobenius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-exact-higher-frobenius)): Let $\mathcal C$ be a finite symmetric tensor category over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. There are an integer $r\ge1$ and an embedding $E_r\hookrightarrow U(k)$ for which the construction of Section <a href="#sec:higher" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:higher">4</a> gives an exact additive strong symmetric monoidal functor $$\Phi:\mathcal C\longrightarrow \mathcal C\boxtimes\operatorname{Ver}_{p^r}(k).$$ It is given by $\Phi(X)=\Theta_{\mathcal C}(X^{\otimes p^r})$, where $\Theta$ is the functor in <a href="#eq:theta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:theta">[eq:theta]</a> for the chosen embedding. The functor $\Phi$ satisfies $\Phi(af)=a^{p^r}\Phi(f)$, and its evaluation at every projective $Q\in\mathcal C$ is given by the natural isomorphism <a href="#eq:phi-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:phi-evaluation">[eq:phi-evaluation]</a>.
  - Explanation: There is a rank and an embedding for which the higher Frobenius construction is an exact additive strong symmetric monoidal functor into the Deligne product with a higher Verlinde category.
- **lem-ordinary-congruence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-congruence)): In the commutative ring $K_0(\mathcal C)/pK_0(\mathcal C)$, one has $$[Y]^p=\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}i[F_i(Y)].$$ Consequently, for every $r\geq1$, $$\label{eq:iterated-ordinary-congruence} [Y]^{p^r} =\sum_{i_1,\ldots,i_r=1}^{p-1} (i_1\cdots i_r) [F_{i_r}\cdots F_{i_1}(Y)] \pmod p.$$
  - Explanation: Modulo p in the Grothendieck ring, the p-th power of a class is a weighted sum of the ordinary Frobenius components, and iterating gives the same for higher powers.
- **lem-equality-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-equality-descent)): Let $S$ be a simple object of $\mathcal C$, and let $A\boxtimes B$ be a composition factor of $\Phi(S)$. Then $d(A)\leq d(S)$. If equality holds, then $$\Phi(S)\simeq A\boxtimes B,\qquad B\text{ is invertible}, \qquad S\in\mathcal C_{\mathrm{ex}}.$$ In this case $A$ is the unique first component of a composition factor of $\Phi(S)$.
  - Explanation: The first component of a composition factor of the image of a simple object has no larger dimension; equality forces the image to be a single factor with invertible second part and the simple to be Frobenius exact.
- **cor-incompressible-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-incompressible-classification)): Let $k$ be algebraically closed of characteristic $p>0$. A finite symmetric tensor category over $k$ is incompressible if and only if it is equivalent as a $k$-linear symmetric tensor category to a category in the corresponding row: $$\begin{array}{c\|l} p=2& \operatorname{Ver}_{2^m}(k)\ (m\ge1),\quad \operatorname{Ver}_{2^m}^+(k)\ (m\ge2),\\[2pt] p=3& \operatorname{Ver}_{3^m}(k),\ \operatorname{Ver}_{3^m}^+(k)\quad(m\ge1),\\[2pt] p\ge5& \operatorname{Vec}_k,\ \operatorname{sVec}_k,\quad \operatorname{Ver}_{p^m}(k),\ \operatorname{Ver}_{p^m}^+(k)\quad(m\ge1). \end{array}$$
  - Explanation: Finite incompressible symmetric tensor categories are exactly the listed vector, supervector and higher Verlinde categories, depending on the characteristic.
- **cor-characteristic-two-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-characteristic-two-uniqueness)): Let $k$ be algebraically closed of characteristic two and let $\mathcal C$ be a finite symmetric tensor category over $k$. There is exactly one fiber functor $$\mathcal C\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ver}_{2^\infty}(k)$$ up to monoidal natural isomorphism. Its isomorphism class contains a representative that factors through $\operatorname{Ver}_{2^n}(k)$ for some finite $n\ge1$. For each fixed $n\ge1$, there is at most one fiber functor $\mathcal C\to\operatorname{Ver}_{2^n}(k)$ up to monoidal natural isomorphism.
  - Explanation: In characteristic two the fiber functor into the tower union is unique up to monoidal isomorphism, and uniqueness holds at each finite stage.
- **cor-generalized-tannaka** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-generalized-tannaka)): Let $k$ be algebraically closed of characteristic $p>0$, and let $\mathcal C$ be a finite symmetric tensor category over $k$. Choose $n\ge1$ and a fiber functor $F:\mathcal C\to\mathcal V=\operatorname{Ver}_{p^n}(k)$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. There is a $\mathcal V$-group $(G,\phi)$ and a $k$-linear symmetric tensor equivalence $$E:\mathcal C\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{Rep}(G,\phi)$$ with a monoidal natural isomorphism $\omega\circ E\cong F$. One may take $$G=\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}^{\otimes}(F),\qquad \phi:\pi(\mathcal V)\longrightarrow \underline{\mathrm{Aut}}^{\otimes}(F)$$ to be the canonical homomorphism induced by $F$.
  - Explanation: A finite symmetric tensor category is equivalent to representations of an internal affine group scheme over the chosen higher Verlinde level, compatibly with the fiber functor.

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-exact-higher-frobenius",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal C$ be a finite symmetric tensor category over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. There are an integer $r\\ge1$ and an embedding $E_r\\hookrightarrow U(k)$ for which the construction of Section <a href=\"#sec:higher\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:higher\">4</a> gives an exact additive strong symmetric monoidal functor $$\\Phi:\\mathcal C\\longrightarrow\n \\mathcal C\\boxtimes\\operatorname{Ver}_{p^r}(k).$$ It is given by $\\Phi(X)=\\Theta_{\\mathcal C}(X^{\\otimes p^r})$, where $\\Theta$ is the functor in <a href=\"#eq:theta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta\">[eq:theta]</a> for the chosen embedding. The functor $\\Phi$ satisfies $\\Phi(af)=a^{p^r}\\Phi(f)$, and its evaluation at every projective $Q\\in\\mathcal C$ is given by the natural isomorphism <a href=\"#eq:phi-evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:phi-evaluation\">[eq:phi-evaluation]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-208.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-exact-higher-frobenius",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal C$ be a finite symmetric tensor category over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. The functor $\\Phi$ satisfies $\\Phi(af)=a^{p^r}\\Phi(f)$, and its evaluation at every projective $Q\\in\\mathcal C$ is given by the natural isomorphism <a href=\"#eq:phi-evaluation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:phi-evaluation\">[eq:phi-evaluation]</a>.",

There are an integer $r\\ge1$ and an embedding $E_r\\hookrightarrow U(k)$ for which the construction of Section <a href=\"#sec:higher\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:higher\">4</a> gives an exact additive strong symmetric monoidal functor $$\\Phi:\\mathcal C\\longrightarrow\n \\mathcal C\\boxtimes\\operatorname{Ver}_{p^r}(k).$$ It is given by $\\Phi(X)=\\Theta_{\\mathcal C}(X^{\\otimes p^r})$, where $\\Theta$ is the functor in <a href=\"#eq:theta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:theta\">[eq:theta]</a> for the chosen embedding.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-congruence",
        "exact_quote": "In the commutative ring $K_0(\\mathcal C)/pK_0(\\mathcal C)$, one has $$[Y]^p=\\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}i[F_i(Y)].$$ Consequently, for every $r\\geq1$, $$\\label{eq:iterated-ordinary-congruence}\n [Y]^{p^r}\n =\\sum_{i_1,\\ldots,i_r=1}^{p-1}\n    (i_1\\cdots i_r)\n    [F_{i_r}\\cdots F_{i_1}(Y)]\n \\pmod p.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-208.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-congruence",
          "exact_quote": "In the commutative ring $K_0(\\mathcal C)/pK_0(\\mathcal C)$, one has $$[Y]^p=\\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}i[F_i(Y)].$$ Consequently, for every $r\\geq1$, $$\\label{eq:iterated-ordinary-congruence}\n [Y]^{p^r}\n =\\sum_{i_1,\\ldots,i_r=1}^{p-1}\n    (i_1\\cdots i_r)\n    [F_{i_r}\\cdots F_{i_1}(Y)]\n \\pmod p.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-208.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In the commutative ring $K_0(\\mathcal C)/pK_0(\\mathcal C)$, one has $$[Y]^p=\\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}i[F_i(Y)].$$ Consequently, for every $r\\geq1$, $$\\label{eq:iterated-ordinary-congruence}\n [Y]^{p^r}\n =\\sum_{i_1,\\ldots,i_r=1}^{p-1}\n    (i_1\\cdots i_r)\n    [F_{i_r}\\cdots F_{i_1}(Y)]\n \\pmod p.$$",
      "explanation": "Modulo p in the Grothendieck ring, the p-th power of a class is a weighted sum of the ordinary Frobenius components, and iterating gives the same for higher powers.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-incompressible-classification",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be algebraically closed of characteristic $p>0$. A finite symmetric tensor category over $k$ is incompressible if and only if it is equivalent as a $k$-linear symmetric tensor category to a category in the corresponding row: $$\\begin{array}{c|l}\np=2&\n \\operatorname{Ver}_{2^m}(k)\\ (m\\ge1),\\quad\n \\operatorname{Ver}_{2^m}^+(k)\\ (m\\ge2),\\\\[2pt]\np=3&\n \\operatorname{Ver}_{3^m}(k),\\\n \\operatorname{Ver}_{3^m}^+(k)\\quad(m\\ge1),\\\\[2pt]\np\\ge5&\n \\operatorname{Vec}_k,\\ \\operatorname{sVec}_k,\\quad\n \\operatorname{Ver}_{p^m}(k),\\\n \\operatorname{Ver}_{p^m}^+(k)\\quad(m\\ge1).\n\\end{array}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-208.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-incompressible-classification",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be algebraically closed of characteristic $p>0$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **finite symmetric tensor category** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *finite symmetric tensor category over $k$* is an essentially small $k$-linear abelian rigid symmetric monoidal category with biexact tensor product, finite-dimensional morphism spaces, finite-length objects, finitely many isomorphism classes of simple objects, enough projectives, and $\operatorname{End}(\mathbf1)=k$.
- **fiber functor** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *fiber functor* between such categories is a $k$-linear exact faithful strong symmetric monoidal functor.
- **incompressible category** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A finite symmetric tensor category is *incompressible* if every $k$-linear exact symmetric tensor functor out of it is fully faithful.
- **tilting module** ([source](#sec:restricted)): A module is *tilting* if it has both a filtration by costandard modules and a filtration by Weyl modules.
- **Steinberg module** ([source](#sec:restricted)): Write $L(h)$ for the simple rational $G$-module of highest weight $h\geq0$, and $\operatorname{St}_m=L(p^m-1)$ for the $m$th Steinberg module; in particular $\operatorname{St}_0=k$.
- **degree of a unipotent module** ([source](#subsec:geometric-lift)): For a finite-dimensional rational $U$-module $D$, its *degree* is the largest degree of an entry of its action matrix $\rho_D(u(t))$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-finite-conventions** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-conventions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-conventions): Unit argument, then a sum of projective covers of simples gives a projective generator. -> simple unit and projective generator
- **step-combine-functors** [claim](#stmt-lem-combine-functors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-combine-functors): Extend multilinear functor by universal property, then dualise for left exactness. -> exact functor on the Deligne product
- **step-bounded-origin** [claim](#stmt-prop-bounded-origin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bounded-origin): Lift to the additive group, take a torsor bundle, extend by reflexive sheaf, bound weights. -> equivariant bundle with small origin weights
- **step-bounded-tilting** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-tilting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-tilting): Reduce to simple modules and induct using Steinberg tensor product and Donkin formula. -> tilting tensor product
- **step-formal-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-formal-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-formal-comparison): Equivariant trivialisation on infinitesimal neighbourhoods via Ext vanishing, then descent to the base field. -> isomorphism of unipotent fibres
- **step-restricted-tilting** [claim](#stmt-thm-restricted-tilting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-formal-comparison-2): Combine the bounded-origin bundle, bounded tilting lemma and formal comparison. -> restricted tilting statement
- **step-theta-detection** [claim](#stmt-lem-theta-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-theta-detection): Stable restriction, faithful tensoring and the tensor identity. -> detection by Theta
- **step-projective-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-lem-projective-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projective-evaluation): Projectives are summands of free modules and additive functors preserve split idempotents. -> natural evaluation isomorphism
- **step-higher-exactness** [claim](#stmt-prop-higher-exactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-higher-exactness): Tensoring by a projective splits sequences; tensoring by a nonzero object reflects exactness. -> exactness criterion
- **step-norm-projectivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-norm-projectivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-norm-projectivity): Local self-injective algebra: socle argument splits off free summands. -> norm criterion for projectivity
- **step-internal-norm-products** [claim](#stmt-lem-internal-norm-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-internal-norm-products): Evaluate norms, use exact faithfulness and product decomposition of norms. -> projectivity of evaluated tensor products
- **step-first-nonprojective** [claim](#stmt-prop-first-nonprojective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-first-nonprojective): Contradiction using Chouinard's theorem, growth count and nonzero tensor ideal containing a symmetrizer. -> least nonprojective rank
- **step-support-independent** [claim](#stmt-lem-support-independent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support-independent): Tensor with an induced module and compute its support as a hyperplane. -> independent support coordinates
- **step-steinberg-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-steinberg-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-steinberg-support): Direct computation on the first Steinberg module and its twists, then tensor-product formula. -> support equations
- **step-moore-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-moore-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moore-selection): Kernel of a Moore-type matrix; additive polynomials bound the rank and give independence. -> independent lambda solving the equations
- **step-support-selection** [claim](#stmt-prop-support-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-support-selection): Pick a support point, solve for the embedding, apply the tensor-product theorem. -> good embedding
- **step-exact-higher-frobenius** [claim](#stmt-thm-exact-higher-frobenius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-exact-higher-frobenius): Minimal rank, good embedding, restricted tilting and detection give nonvanishing, then exactness criterion. -> exact higher Frobenius functor
- **step-ordinary-congruence** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-congruence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-congruence): Filtration of the p-th tensor power and iteration in the Grothendieck ring modulo p. -> congruence for powers
- **step-equality-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-equality-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-equality-descent): Dimension inequalities, evaluation at a projective cover, and the congruence. -> dimension descent and Frobenius exactness
- **step-main-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#subsec:completion): Iterate the functor on a finite graph, fibre the Serre closure over a low Verlinde level, combine factors and correct scalar twist. -> fiber functor to a finite higher Verlinde category
- **step-incompressible-classification** [claim](#stmt-cor-incompressible-classification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-incompressible-classification): Fiber functor and dimension inequalities give an equivalence onto a subcategory; use the subcategory classification. -> classification list
- **step-characteristic-two-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-cor-characteristic-two-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-characteristic-two-uniqueness): Existence from the main theorem, uniqueness from a cited subterminality result, lift through fully faithful inclusion. -> uniqueness statements
- **step-generalized-tannaka** [claim](#stmt-cor-generalized-tannaka) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-generalized-tannaka): Apply generalized Tannaka duality to the fiber functor. -> reconstruction of the category

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-existence**: Every finite symmetric tensor category over an algebraically closed field of positive characteristic admits a fiber functor into some finite higher Verlinde category. The level may depend on the category.
- **inv-norm-projectivity**: For a finite p-group H, a finite-dimensional kH-module is projective exactly when its norm map is an isomorphism.
- **inv-first-nonprojective**: A least rank exists at which the evaluated tensor-power module is nonprojective. At that rank its restriction to every proper subgroup is projective.
- **inv-char2-uniqueness**: In characteristic two the fiber functor into the tower union is unique up to monoidal natural isomorphism, and at most one exists at each fixed finite level.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-nonfinite-categories**: Apply the main theorem to infinite symmetric tensor categories of moderate growth. -> The argument needs a projective generator and finitely many simple classes, so nothing is asserted outside the finite case.
- **anti-prescribed-level**: Assume the fiber functor exists at a Verlinde level fixed in advance. -> Existence is only at some level depending on the category, so a prescribed level may fail.
- **anti-finite-type-group**: Assume the reconstructed internal group scheme is finite or of finite type. -> Only affineness is asserted, so finiteness-based algorithms are unjustified.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements. The fiber functor exists at some level depending on the category, and the least nonprojective rank is obtained by a contradiction argument with no finite procedure. The source says the existence theorem supplies some finite height depending on the category, so there is no finite computation to run.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence of a fiber functor and gives no program or data format. The functor is assembled from a semilinear tensor-power construction with several choices (an embedding, a number of iterations, a tower level). The paper does not make these choices computable. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results are structural statements about symmetric tensor categories in positive characteristic. They have no dynamics, state evolution, randomness or physical model to simulate. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool or checker is specified. Tannakian reconstruction is stated for categories and affine group schemes in ind-completions, and no finiteness property of the reconstructed group is claimed, so no finite tool input is available. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only combinatorial step is a pigeonhole argument on a finite directed graph of simple objects, used inside a proof. It is not a procedure with inputs and outputs, and the proofs rely on existence arguments (least rank, support points, Moore-matrix kernels) that are not effective as stated. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite-algebra description of a category as modules over an endomorphism algebra is a theoretical identification, not a storage scheme with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns circuits, arithmetic units or parallel execution. The objects are abstract exact functors and tilting modules over an algebraically closed field. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The work is carried out over an algebraically closed field of positive characteristic, which is not a finite-field setting with a hardness assumption or a security notion. No scheme, adversary or reduction appears. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss, model class or learning guarantee. The classification of incompressible categories is purely algebraic. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective function, constraint set or optimum is studied. The least rank where the tensor-power module stops being projective is an existence statement about modules, not an optimisation problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No processes, messages, faults or consistency conditions occur. The symmetric monoidal structure concerns commutativity of tensor products of objects, not concurrent computation. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The contribution is pure representation theory and category theory (restricted tilting, higher Frobenius functor, dimension descent, classification and Tannakian reconstruction). The level n is existential and depends on the category, so no engineering contract can be extracted. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-finite-category** (hypothesis): The main theorem is proved only for finite symmetric tensor categories, where the projective generator and the finite set of simple classes are essential. No assertion is made for arbitrary nonfinite categories of moderate growth. Blocked use: ['Applying the fiber functor existence theorem to infinite symmetric tensor categories of moderate growth']
- **limit-level-not-prescribed** (nonconstructive): The existence theorem supplies some finite height depending on the category. It does not supply existence at every prescribed height. Blocked use: ['Fixing the Verlinde level n in advance independently of the category']
- **limit-group-scheme-finiteness** (formal_scope): In the reconstruction the group scheme is only asserted to be affine. No finiteness or finite-type property is used or claimed. Blocked use: ['Assuming the reconstructed internal group scheme is finite or of finite type']
- **limit-positive-characteristic** (hypothesis): All results are over an algebraically closed field of positive characteristic. The higher Verlinde tower and the Frobenius constructions are not available in characteristic zero, where the common target is supervector spaces. Blocked use: ['Using the construction over fields of characteristic zero or fields that are not algebraically closed']
- **limit-uniqueness-char-two** (formal_scope): Uniqueness of the fiber functor is asserted only in characteristic two, in the tower union and at each fixed finite level where one exists. Blocked use: ['Claiming uniqueness of fiber functors in odd characteristic']
- **limit-least-rank-existence** (nonconstructive): The least rank at which the evaluated tensor-power module is nonprojective is obtained by contradiction. It is not given by a finite procedure. Blocked use: ['Reading the least rank as the output of a computable routine']
- **limit-ordinary-frobenius-not-exact** (edge_case): The ordinary Frobenius functor need not be exact. Exactness holds only on the Frobenius-exact subcategory, so the descent argument cannot treat it as exact on the whole category. Blocked use: ['Applying the ordinary Frobenius functor as an exact functor on a general category']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the finite case of the Benson–Etingof–Ostrik conjecture: every finite symmetric tensor category over an algebraically closed field <i>k</i> of characteristic <i>p</i> &gt; 0 admits a <i>k</i>-linear exact faithful strong symmetric monoidal functor to a finite higher Verlinde category $`\mathop{\mathrm{Ver}}\nolimits _{p^n}(k)`$. The result includes characteristic two, and the level <i>n</i> may depend on the category.
